  "old_string": "The Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: the \\emph{direct} limits voting diversity ($K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$), while the \\emph{indirect} collapses MAX-SAT to vote counting (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\\times$ LLaMA) aligns with regime boundaries; thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) confirms this scales with capability. For practitioners, $C_0$ with SC accuracy predicts when augmentation will fail. The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}. If traces do not reflect actual reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. An alternative $\\sigma(c, a)$ based on Z3 semantic evaluation---testing satisfiability under answer $a$ regardless of provenance---could in principle avoid degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), but faces circular dependency (evaluating constraints requires encoding what each answer implies) and converges empirically at $\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$. <details open><summary>🔧 Tool call — Edit · `toolu_01VpgysKkQu6esszzEFWrjb5`</summary>

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/conclusion.tex",
  "old_string": "We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted logical constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$, 83.3\\% of constraints on incorrect problems support the wrong answer), explaining why twenty-plus remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp) confirm the verification mechanism is sound; the failure is in self-extraction. This carries a broader implication: any self-verification pipeline that derives checking criteria from the same model's outputs risks the same independence bottleneck, regardless of whether the verification channel is formal or neural.\n\nThree reusable diagnostics---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---predict when trace-aggregation will fail. Practitioners should evaluate $C_0$ and SC accuracy before deploying symbolic augmentation: $C_0 < 5\\%$ guarantees SICA reduces to SC, and SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification.\n\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage on two of four generators (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$), suggesting that architectural diversity---not additional same-model computation---provides the path forward. Open questions include extraction temperature sensitivity, scaling behavior beyond 32B, and whether trained task-specific verifiers can further close the gap between zero-shot NLI and the oracle ceiling.",
  "new_string": "We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted logical constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$, 83.3\\% on incorrect problems), explaining why twenty-plus remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the failure is in self-extraction. More broadly, any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model risks the same bottleneck, whether the channel is formal or neural. Three reusable diagnostics---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---predict when trace-aggregation will fail; practitioners should evaluate $C_0$ and SC accuracy first ($C_0 < 5\\%$: SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$: error amplification risk). A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage on two of four generators (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$), suggesting architectural diversity as the path forward. Open questions include extraction temperature sensitivity, scaling beyond 32B, and whether trained verifiers can close the NLI--oracle gap."
}
```

Three reusable diagnostics---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---predict when trace-aggregation will fail; practitioners should evaluate $C_0$ and SC accuracy first ($C_0 < 5\\%$: SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$: error amplification risk). A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage on two of four generators (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$), suggesting architectural diversity as the path forward. Open questions include extraction temperature sensitivity, scaling beyond 32B, and whether trained verifiers can close the NLI--oracle gap."
        ]
      }
    ],

  },

```text
41	
42	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
43	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
44	
45	Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Consequently, 94\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\% of constraints on average, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}).
46	
47	The causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts logical constraints consistent with it. Constraints use trace-specific variable names (56.4\% zero cross-answer variable overlap), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all and aggregation reduces to weighted majority voting. Confirmation bias generalizes across families: $4.97\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\times$ (Qwen-14B), and $2.88\times$ (LLaMA), all exceeding $1.0$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Bias intensifies along reasoning steps: late-step constraints (step $\geq 7$) exhibit 27.2\% conclusion leakage vs.\ 22.0\% for early steps (Fisher $p = 3 \times 10^{-6}$); SICA restricted to late-only constraints degrades by $-10.3$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}).
48	
49	
50	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
51	\label{sec:process-kappa}
52	
53	Answer-level $\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:
54	\begin{equation}
55	\label{eq:process-sim}
56	\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \sum_{i < j} \cos(\mathbf{v}_i^q, \mathbf{v}_j^q),
57	\end{equation}
58	where $\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Appendix Figure~\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.
59	
60	
61	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}
62	\label{sec:diagnostics}
63	
64	Further diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\sim}$10\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant improvement ($p = 0.013$), indicating that the formal verification mechanism itself adds value---but this gain is overwhelmed by confirmation bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$).
65	
66	
67	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
68	\label{sec:remediation}
69	\label{sec:interventions}
70	
```

### Tool result

_source line 16028 · 2026-05-21T03:54:18.472Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_019toKGQELT3CHbyS7sYWYaM`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$); TOST equivalence tests (margin $\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for five conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the oracle ceiling.
8	Cross-architecture NLI verification yields significant gains on two of four generators from distinct model families (Mistral-7B $+3.50$\,pp, $p{=}0.013$; LLaMA-3.1-8B $+4.17$\,pp, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), with gain magnitude predicted by the verifier--generator capability gap.
9	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_0162FRNmc8gwENVZeqHXVRQX`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.
4	The dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.
5	SC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.
6	\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.
7	The gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.
8	
9	Confidence-weighted \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.
10	Recent neuro-symbolic methods \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.
11	A simpler question precedes these: can the \emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?
12	No prior work has systematically evaluated this.
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}
5	SC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
8	Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\sim}$40\% translation errors. SatLM \citep{ye2024satlm} generates complete SAT formulations from problem descriptions and reports positive results; critically, SatLM's constraints derive from \emph{problem-level} understanding, whereas SICA extracts from \emph{reasoning traces}. Problem-level formalization avoids answer contamination; trace-level extraction inherits confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Program-aided approaches \citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} parallel the self-extraction bottleneck. Recent extensions \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent} each introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}
11	Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.
12	
13	\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification.
15	Prior self-correction studies \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) measures independent information across $K$ traces; the bias ratio (BR; Eq.~\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies post-hoc rationalization, connecting to CoT faithfulness concerns \citep{turpin2024language}; conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates step-level contamination. Together, these show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides a practical decision criterion: combo gain decreases monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$).
```

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted logical constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\% on incorrect problems), explaining why twenty-plus remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the failure is in self-extraction. More broadly, any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model risks the same bottleneck, whether the channel is formal or neural. Three reusable diagnostics---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---predict when trace-aggregation will fail; practitioners should evaluate $C_0$ and SC accuracy first ($C_0 < 5\%$: SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$: error amplification risk). A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage on two of four generators (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$), suggesting architectural diversity as the path forward. Open questions include extraction temperature sensitivity, scaling beyond 32B, and whether trained verifiers can close the NLI--oracle gap.
4	
```

</details>

69	\label{sec:interventions}
70	
71	Twenty-five-plus remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \ref{tab:remediation}, \ref{tab:additional_interventions}). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
72	
73	\begin{figure}[t]
74	\centering
75	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}

77	\label{fig:interventions-summary}
78	\end{figure}

      "content": "\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nTwenty-five-plus remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}",

    "oldString": "Twenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families",
    "newString": "Twenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families",
    "originalFile": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$); TOST equivalence tests (margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp) establish equivalence for five conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.\nWe identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.\nProcess-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.\nTwenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.\nCross-architecture NLI verification yields significant gains on two of four generators from distinct model families (Mistral-7B $+3.50$\\,pp, $p{=}0.013$; LLaMA-3.1-8B $+4.17$\\,pp, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), with gain magnitude predicted by the verifier--generator capability gap.\n",

          "+Twenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted logical constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$, 83.3\\% on incorrect problems), explaining why twenty-plus remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the failure is in self-extraction. More broadly, any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model risks the same bottleneck, whether the channel is formal or neural. Three reusable diagnostics---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---predict when trace-aggregation will fail; practitioners should evaluate $C_0$ and SC accuracy first ($C_0 < 5\\%$: SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$: error amplification risk). A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage on two of four generators (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$), suggesting architectural diversity as the path forward. Open questions include extraction temperature sensitivity, scaling beyond 32B, and whether trained verifiers can close the NLI--oracle gap.\n",

    "originalFile": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$); TOST equivalence tests (margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp) establish equivalence for five conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.\nWe identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.\nProcess-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.\nTwenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.\nCross-architecture NLI verification yields significant gains on two of four generators from distinct model families (Mistral-7B $+3.50$\\,pp, $p{=}0.013$; LLaMA-3.1-8B $+4.17$\\,pp, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), with gain magnitude predicted by the verifier--generator capability gap.\n",

          " Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.",
          "-Cross-architecture NLI verification yields significant gains on two of four generators from distinct model families (Mistral-7B $+3.50$\\,pp, $p{=}0.013$; LLaMA-3.1-8B $+4.17$\\,pp, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), with gain magnitude predicted by the verifier--generator capability gap.",

{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "097ec7b3-7b5a-4dcf-bb40-daf6a3e40886",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "1cfc31c5-0adf-491d-946f-9ebe5e43d144",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "ebc75e23-cd03-49f4-bd1d-028e7b8175dc",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "oldString": "Twenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families",
    "newString": "Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract logical constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nTwenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck persists at the 7B--32B scale.\nPrior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \\emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case. We show that even formal verification inherits the model's bias, revealing process-level homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier yields significant gains on two of four generators across model families ($+3.50$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $p{=}0.013$; $+4.17$\\,pp LLaMA-3.1-8B, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), suggesting the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap is largest for weaker models and narrows with capability, but process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",

We show that even formal verification inherits the model's bias, revealing process-level homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.",

We show that even formal verification inherits the model's bias, revealing process-level homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier yields significant gains on two of four generators across model families ($+3.50$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $p{=}0.013$; $+4.17$\\,pp LLaMA-3.1-8B, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), suggesting the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap is largest for weaker models and narrows with capability, but process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nSelf-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints on average, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts logical constraints consistent with it. Constraints use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% zero cross-answer variable overlap), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all and aggregation reduces to weighted majority voting. Confirmation bias generalizes across families: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all exceeding $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Bias intensifies along reasoning steps: late-step constraints (step $\\geq 7$) exhibit 27.2\\% conclusion leakage vs.\\ 22.0\\% for early steps (Fisher $p = 3 \\times 10^{-6}$); SICA restricted to late-only constraints degrades by $-10.3$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant improvement ($p = 0.013$), indicating that the formal verification mechanism itself adds value---but this gain is overwhelmed by confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nTwenty-five-plus remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 22+ intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}",

SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nSelf-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints on average, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts logical constraints consistent with it. Constraints use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% zero cross-answer variable overlap), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all and aggregation reduces to weighted majority voting. Confirmation bias generalizes across families: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all exceeding $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Bias intensifies along reasoning steps: late-step constraints (step $\\geq 7$) exhibit 27.2\\% conclusion leakage vs.\\ 22.0\\% for early steps (Fisher $p = 3 \\times 10^{-6}$); SICA restricted to late-only constraints degrades by $-10.3$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant improvement ($p = 0.013$), indicating that the formal verification mechanism itself adds value---but this gain is overwhelmed by confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nTwenty-seven remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 22+ intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}",

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   This session handles multiple sequential Director instructions for the symb_invariant_consensus EMNLP 2026 paper (DDL May 25):

   **Completed: CRITICAL w-value fix** — Fixed selective reporting where Abstract and Intro mixed w=3 and w=5 peak results. Changed to w=3 primary values throughout (Mistral +3.50pp p=0.013, LLaMA +4.17pp p=0.0035).

   **Completed: BH correction integration** — Integrated Benjamini-Hochberg FDR correction (m=16, 0/16 significant after correction) across 8 locations. Two conditions that were "significant" at raw level (Mistral PW p=0.014, Qwen2.5 PW p=0.011) now annotated as "nominally significant" with BH adj. p=0.112.

   **Completed: TOST count sync** — Updated abstract "four conditions" → "five conditions" to match experiments.tex and appendix TOST section (5 equivalence / 5 genuine / 6 underpowered = 16 total).

   **Completed: Conclusion expansion + narrative audit** — Expanded conclusion from ~85 to ~148 words (single paragraph). Added: field implications (self-verification bottleneck is general), practical guidance (C0+SC diagnostics), cross-arch promise, open questions. Compressed Discussion to make room. Narrative audit confirmed all numbers consistent across all sections.

   **In Progress: Abstract/Intro framing polish** — Unified remediation count to "twenty-seven" across all files (verified by appendix "27 distinct approaches"). Added Spearman ρ=0.970 to abstract's capability gap claim. Assessed intro hook (adequate), SatLM distinction (sharp), §5 SCB presentation (adequate) — no changes needed for these. Compiled successfully but page 8/9 boundary not yet visually verified for this latest edit round.

        "content": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nSelf-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints on average, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts logical constraints consistent with it. Constraints use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% zero cross-answer variable overlap), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all and aggregation reduces to weighted majority voting. Confirmation bias generalizes across families: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all exceeding $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Bias intensifies along reasoning steps: late-step constraints (step $\\geq 7$) exhibit 27.2\\% conclusion leakage vs.\\ 22.0\\% for early steps (Fisher $p = 3 \\times 10^{-6}$); SICA restricted to late-only constraints degrades by $-10.3$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant improvement ($p = 0.013$), indicating that the formal verification mechanism itself adds value---but this gain is overwhelmed by confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nTwenty-seven remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}",

{
  "parentUuid": "975a04f4-fc1d-43a9-a910-800db18ea54d",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract logical constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nTwenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck persists at the 7B--32B scale.\nPrior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \\emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case. We show that even formal verification inherits the model's bias, revealing process-level homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-seven remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier yields significant gains on two of four generators across model families ($+3.50$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $p{=}0.013$; $+4.17$\\,pp LLaMA-3.1-8B, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), suggesting the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap is largest for weaker models and narrows with capability, but process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",
        "numLines": 78,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 78
      }
    },

      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
        "content": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$); TOST equivalence tests (margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp) establish equivalence for five conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.\nWe identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.\nProcess-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.\nTwenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.\nCross-architecture NLI verification yields significant gains on two of four generators from distinct model families (Mistral-7B $+3.50$\\,pp, $p{=}0.013$; LLaMA-3.1-8B $+4.17$\\,pp, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), with gain magnitude predicted by the verifier--generator capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$).\n",
        "numLines": 9,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 9
      }
    },

        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nThe Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\\times$ LLaMA) confirms this scales with capability; thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) marks the upper bound. The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}. If traces do not reflect actual reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. An alternative $\\sigma(c, a)$ via Z3 semantic evaluation could in principle avoid degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), but faces circular dependency and converges at $\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$.\n",
        "numLines": 5,

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	The Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\times$ LLaMA) confirms this scales with capability; thinking-mode degeneration ($\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) marks the upper bound. The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}. If traces do not reflect actual reasoning \citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. An alternative $\sigma(c, a)$ via Z3 semantic evaluation could in principle avoid degeneration (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}), but faces circular dependency and converges at $\text{BR} = 4.97\times$.
5	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_017WunxTvLdHguk6zXxmT2UK`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted logical constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\% on incorrect problems), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the failure is in self-extraction. More broadly, any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model risks the same bottleneck, whether the channel is formal or neural. Three reusable diagnostics---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---predict when trace-aggregation will fail; practitioners should evaluate $C_0$ and SC accuracy first ($C_0 < 5\%$: SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$: error amplification risk). A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage on two of four generators (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$), suggesting architectural diversity as the path forward. Open questions include extraction temperature sensitivity, scaling beyond 32B, and whether trained verifiers can close the NLI--oracle gap.
4	
```

</details>

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
I need to trim about one line from the conclusion. Let me compress some phrases:

Current text (approximately):
"We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted logical constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\% on incorrect problems), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the failure is in self-extraction. 

The core insight generalizes beyond this specific setup—any self-verification system that derives its checking criteria from the same model faces this same bottleneck, whether through formal methods or neural approaches. I've identified three diagnostic metrics that can predict when this will fail, and practitioners should prioritize checking the baseline accuracy and self-consistency rates first. A cross-architecture experiment shows we can escape this trap on some models, with strong correlation between predictions and outcomes, suggesting that architectural diversity might be the solution. There are still open questions around temperature sensitivity during extraction, whether this scales to larger models, and if we can train separate verifiers to bridge the gap between the neural and oracle performance." I'm working through line-saving edits across several key claims. Removing "whether the channel is formal or neural" trims the self-verification insight, swapping "suggesting architectural diversity as the path forward" for "pointing to architectural diversity" tightens the proof-of-concept finding, and collapsing "extraction temperature sensitivity" saves another word. These targeted cuts should reclaim roughly a full line without losing the core argument.
```

  "content": "[Director 指令] [Critical Polish] Discussion 扩展 + Intro 差异化加强\n\n**背景**：Reviewer 模拟 R2 审稿发现 Discussion 仅 4 句话（~90 words），是 3.0→3.5 的最大障碍。这是当前 ROI 最高的修改，不需要新实验。\n\n**任务 1（最高优先级）：Discussion 扩展到 3-4 段（0.75-1.0 页）**\n\n扩展方向（全部基于现有数据，不要虚构）：\n\n**段 1 — Self-verification 一般化**：\nBR>1 不仅适用于 FOL extraction，也预测 NL self-verification 的失败。连接 Huang et al. 2024 和 Stechly et al. 2025：他们定性观察到 self-correction 无效，我们的 BR=4.97× 定量解释了为什么——self-extracted verification criteria 系统性偏向模型自身答案。\"any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model risks the same bottleneck\"（目前只在 Conclusion 一句话，需要在 Discussion 展开论证）。\n\n**段 2 — Test-time compute scaling 含义**：\nK_eff=1.2-3.6 for nominal K=12 意味着增加 sampling budget 的收益急剧递减。QwQ-32B κ=0.91 (K_eff=1.09) 意味着 reasoning models 的 trace 近乎确定性。对 o1/R1 style 的 long reasoning chain + self-verification：更强的模型产生更一致的 traces，**降低**了同模型聚合的有效性。κ universality 跨 4 families × 3 methods（SC κ=0.44, Debate κ=0.15 但 acc -18.6pp, Diverse-Prompt κ=0.40）表明 \"same-model trace diversity has a floor\"。\n\n**段 3 — Independence-accuracy tradeoff 的 ensemble 理论意义**：\n展开 Krogh & Vetter 1994 引用。Debate 成功打破 κ（0.44→0.15, K_eff 2.0→4.4）但 accuracy 崩塌，这不是 implementation 问题而是 ensemble learning 的基本 tension：同一模型不能同时输出一致且独立的 traces。Cross-architecture escape 之所以 work（Spearman ρ=0.970），正是因为 DeBERTa 和 decoder-only LLM 有结构性不同的 error profiles。\n\n**段 4 — Practitioner implications**：\n连接 Conclusion 的 C0/SC 诊断到 broader 决策：C0<5% → 不值得跑 SICA；SC≤40% → error amplification risk；中等 SC + 有外部 verifier → cross-architecture combo 值得尝试。同时提及 Z3 semantic dedup 的 positive signal (p=0.013) 作为\"mechanism is sound, extraction is bottleneck\"的额外证据。\n\n**页数预算**：当前 Discussion < 0.25 页。扩展到 ~0.75 页。如果超 8 页限制，从 §4 Experiments setup 或 §5 Analysis 中压缩移入 appendix（setup 细节最容易压缩）。\n\n**任务 2：Intro self-correction differentiation 加强**\n\n在 introduction.tex:25（\"Z3 introduces a sound, non-neural channel\"）之后加 2-3 句，从 assertion 升级为 argument：\n\n参考句式（按需调整）：\n\"Unlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints: Z3 can detect inconsistencies between constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger feedback loop still fails—Z3 rejects only 3.4% of constraints (§4.2) because constraints are individually valid yet collectively biased—reveals a failure mode invisible to prior work: the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it's expressed).\"\n\n**任务 3：两个快速补强**\n\nA. **experiments.tex:66 附近加 4-seed anti-cherry-pick 句**：\"Four-seed replication shows that intermediate-SC positive trends reflect seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative Δ, and the aggregate (+3.43pp) does not reach significance (p=0.092).\"\n\nB. **Z3 semantic dedup 扩展**：analysis.tex 中将当前一句话扩展为 2-3 句：\"Z3-based semantic deduplication's positive signal confirms that the formal verification channel provides genuine value when redundancy is reduced; the bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality and independence of input constraints.\"\n\n**硬约束**：主文 ≤8 页（Limitations 豁免）。修完编译确认。"
}
