_source line 16156 · 2026-05-21T04:37:08.202Z_

[Director 指令] [Critical Polish] Discussion 扩展 + Intro 差异化加强

**背景**：Reviewer 模拟 R2 审稿发现 Discussion 仅 4 句话（~90 words），是 3.0→3.5 的最大障碍。这是当前 ROI 最高的修改，不需要新实验。

**任务 1（最高优先级）：Discussion 扩展到 3-4 段（0.75-1.0 页）**

扩展方向（全部基于现有数据，不要虚构）：

**段 1 — Self-verification 一般化**：
BR>1 不仅适用于 FOL extraction，也预测 NL self-verification 的失败。连接 Huang et al. 2024 和 Stechly et al. 2025：他们定性观察到 self-correction 无效，我们的 BR=4.97× 定量解释了为什么——self-extracted verification criteria 系统性偏向模型自身答案。"any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model risks the same bottleneck"（目前只在 Conclusion 一句话，需要在 Discussion 展开论证）。

**段 2 — Test-time compute scaling 含义**：
K_eff=1.2-3.6 for nominal K=12 意味着增加 sampling budget 的收益急剧递减。QwQ-32B κ=0.91 (K_eff=1.09) 意味着 reasoning models 的 trace 近乎确定性。对 o1/R1 style 的 long reasoning chain + self-verification：更强的模型产生更一致的 traces，**降低**了同模型聚合的有效性。κ universality 跨 4 families × 3 methods（SC κ=0.44, Debate κ=0.15 但 acc -18.6pp, Diverse-Prompt κ=0.40）表明 "same-model trace diversity has a floor"。

**段 3 — Independence-accuracy tradeoff 的 ensemble 理论意义**：
展开 Krogh & Vetter 1994 引用。Debate 成功打破 κ（0.44→0.15, K_eff 2.0→4.4）但 accuracy 崩塌，这不是 implementation 问题而是 ensemble learning 的基本 tension：同一模型不能同时输出一致且独立的 traces。Cross-architecture escape 之所以 work（Spearman ρ=0.970），正是因为 DeBERTa 和 decoder-only LLM 有结构性不同的 error profiles。

**段 4 — Practitioner implications**：
连接 Conclusion 的 C0/SC 诊断到 broader 决策：C0<5% → 不值得跑 SICA；SC≤40% → error amplification risk；中等 SC + 有外部 verifier → cross-architecture combo 值得尝试。同时提及 Z3 semantic dedup 的 positive signal (p=0.013) 作为"mechanism is sound, extraction is bottleneck"的额外证据。

**页数预算**：当前 Discussion < 0.25 页。扩展到 ~0.75 页。如果超 8 页限制，从 §4 Experiments setup 或 §5 Analysis 中压缩移入 appendix（setup 细节最容易压缩）。

**任务 2：Intro self-correction differentiation 加强**

在 introduction.tex:25（"Z3 introduces a sound, non-neural channel"）之后加 2-3 句，从 assertion 升级为 argument：

参考句式（按需调整）：
"Unlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints: Z3 can detect inconsistencies between constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger feedback loop still fails—Z3 rejects only 3.4% of constraints (§4.2) because constraints are individually valid yet collectively biased—reveals a failure mode invisible to prior work: the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it's expressed)."

**任务 3：两个快速补强**

A. **experiments.tex:66 附近加 4-seed anti-cherry-pick 句**："Four-seed replication shows that intermediate-SC positive trends reflect seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative Δ, and the aggregate (+3.43pp) does not reach significance (p=0.092)."

B. **Z3 semantic dedup 扩展**：analysis.tex 中将当前一句话扩展为 2-3 句："Z3-based semantic deduplication's positive signal confirms that the formal verification channel provides genuine value when redundancy is reduced; the bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality and independence of input constraints."

**硬约束**：主文 ≤8 页（Limitations 豁免）。修完编译确认。

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted logical constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\% on incorrect problems), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the failure is in self-extraction. More broadly, any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model risks the same bottleneck. Three reusable diagnostics---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---predict when trace-aggregation will fail; practitioners should evaluate $C_0$ and SC accuracy first ($C_0 < 5\%$: SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$: error amplification risk). A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage on two of four generators (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$), pointing to architectural diversity. Open questions include temperature sensitivity, scaling beyond 32B, and whether trained verifiers can close the NLI--oracle gap.
4	
```

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	The Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\times$ LLaMA) confirms this scales with capability; thinking-mode degeneration ($\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) marks the upper bound. The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}. If traces do not reflect actual reasoning \citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. An alternative $\sigma(c, a)$ via Z3 semantic evaluation could in principle avoid degeneration (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}), but faces circular dependency and converges at $\text{BR} = 4.97\times$.
5

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01QH66jQCsmKYvGnqCDRnS52`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.
4	The dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.
5	SC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.
6	\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.
7	The gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.
8	
9	Confidence-weighted \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.
10	Recent neuro-symbolic methods \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.
11	A simpler question precedes these: can the \emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?
12	No prior work has systematically evaluated this.
13	
14	We ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \citep{austensmith1996}.
15	When independence fails \citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.
16	Measuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
17	The bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.
18	Chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
19	When the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
20	Self-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.
21	
22	We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract logical constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
23	We evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
24	Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck persists at the 7B--32B scale.
25	Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case. We show that even formal verification inherits the model's bias, revealing process-level homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.
26	
27	Oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \S\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling.
28	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp; other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling across three families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
29	A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+3.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
30	
31	Our contributions are:
32	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
33	\item \textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
34	
35	\item \textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\kappa \geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-seven remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier yields significant gains on two of four generators across model families ($+3.50$\,pp Mistral-7B, $p{=}0.013$; $+4.17$\,pp LLaMA-3.1-8B, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), suggesting the ceiling is escapable (\S\ref{sec:remediation}, \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
36	
37	\item \textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap is largest for weaker models and narrows with capability, but process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
38	\end{enumerate}
39	
40	\begin{figure}[t]
41	\centering

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Setup}
5	\label{sec:exp-setup}
6	
7	We evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models on three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations across ${\sim}$1,250 unique instances. Beyond accuracy comparisons, we conduct diagnostic analyses of symbolic confirmation bias and process-level trace independence (\S\ref{sec:analysis}).
8	
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$; extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$. Z3 timeout: 10s. $\alpha{=}0.5$. Statistical significance via McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs from 10,000 resamples for $n \geq 200$.
14	
15	\paragraph{Computational cost.} On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($K{=}12$), SICA takes $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock time; constraint extraction ($K$ additional LLM calls) accounts for 76\% of overhead, Z3 solving is negligible (${<}0.1\%$).
16	
17	
18	\subsection{Main Results}
19	\label{sec:main-results}
20	
21	Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline. We identify three empirical regimes (thresholds observation-driven, not theoretically derived). SICA effectiveness depends sharply on this accuracy regime.
22

Results hold across the full capability range: from Mistral-7B (54.41\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86.27\% SC), SICA delivers no significant gain. $\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). $^b$In-context learning with gold logical examples. $\dagger$\,$n < 100$ (suggestive only). \textbf{Bold $\Delta$}: $p < 0.05$ (McNemar). {*}\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$);\enspace{***}\,$p < 0.001$ (oracle). Failure modes detailed in Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}. $^{4s}$\,4-seed replication (confirmatory); unmarked single-seed conditions are exploratory.}

65	
66	None of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation (uncorrected McNemar $p < 0.05$): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} ($\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for 5 conditions; 5 show genuine differences (4 negative); 6 are underpowered (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). We trace the failure to three mechanisms in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\S\ref{sec:remediation}.
67	
68	
69	\subsection{Oracle Control}
70	\label{sec:oracle}
71	
72	Oracle variants replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations: logical formulae from FOLIO ($n = 204$) and DSL from ProofWriter ($n = 600$). Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \times 10^{-7}$), and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). 1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	We test the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:mirage}).
5	
6	\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ produces degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).
10	
11	\begin{table}[t]
12	\centering
13	\small
14	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
15	\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\kappa$ descending). $\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$, 4 traces each.}
16	\label{tab:independence}
17	\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}
18	\toprule
19	Model & Condition & $\kappa$ & $K_{\text{eff}}$ & SC (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
20	\midrule

22	Qwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\
23	Qwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\
24	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\
25	Qwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\

27	Mistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\

39	\end{table}
40	
41	
42	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
43	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
44	
45	Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Consequently, 94\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\% of constraints on average, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}).
46	
47	The causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts logical constraints consistent with it. Constraints use trace-specific variable names (56.4\% zero cross-answer variable overlap), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all and aggregation reduces to weighted majority voting. Confirmation bias generalizes across families: $4.97\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\times$ (Qwen-14B), and $2.88\times$ (LLaMA), all exceeding $1.0$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Bias intensifies along reasoning steps: late-step constraints (step $\geq 7$) exhibit 27.2\% conclusion leakage vs.\ 22.0\% for early steps (Fisher $p = 3 \times 10^{-6}$); SICA restricted to late-only constraints degrades by $-10.3$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}).
48	
49	
50	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
51	\label{sec:process-kappa}
52	
53	Answer-level $\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:
54	\begin{equation}
55	\label{eq:process-sim}
56	\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \sum_{i < j} \cos(\mathbf{v}_i^q, \mathbf{v}_j^q),
57	\end{equation}
58	where $\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Appendix Figure~\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.
59	
60	
61	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}
62	\label{sec:diagnostics}
63	
64	Further diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\sim}$10\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant improvement ($p = 0.013$), indicating that the formal verification mechanism itself adds value---but this gain is overwhelmed by confirmation bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$).
65	
66	
67	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
68	\label{sec:remediation}
69	\label{sec:interventions}
70	
71	Twenty-seven remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \ref{tab:remediation}, \ref{tab:additional_interventions}). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
72	
73	\begin{figure}[t]
74	\centering
75	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}
76	\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\pm 1$\,pp noise zone.}
77	\label{fig:interventions-summary}
78	\end{figure}
```

Bias intensifies along reasoning steps: late-step constraints (step $\\geq 7$) exhibit 27.2\\% conclusion leakage vs.\\ 22.0\\% for early steps (Fisher $p = 3 \\times 10^{-6}$); SICA restricted to late-only constraints degrades by $-10.3$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant improvement ($p = 0.013$), indicating that the formal verification mechanism itself adds value---but this gain is overwhelmed by confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nTwenty-seven remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}",

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	We test this with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \textsc{entailment}, \textsc{contradiction}, or \textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; $w$ is a dataset-specific hyperparameter ($w{=}5$ yields $+10.67$\,pp on Mistral-7B but only $+1.67$\,pp n.s.\ on LLaMA-3.1-8B), and higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. Per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\%) and performs poorly on \textsc{Unknown} (3.5\% accuracy), as NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	The 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \textsc{False} (71.43\% vs.\ SC's 38.10\%), SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures tested (DeBERTa, RoBERTa, BART) produce significant improvements at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}).
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
15	We evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with the identical DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}): Mistral-7B (SC${=}$39.33\%, gap${=}{+}15.67$) gains $+3.50$\,pp ($p{=}0.013$) and LLaMA-3.1-8B (SC${=}$70.00\%, gap${=}{-}15.00$) achieves $+4.17$\,pp ($p{=}0.0035$), while Qwen2.5-14B and Qwen3-14B plateau at ${\leq}1.00$\,pp (n.s.). NLI escape persists even when the verifier is moderately weaker in standalone accuracy, but vanishes as the capability gap widens (${\leq}{-}18.5$\,pp). Sigmoid and linear fits yield similar adjusted $R^2$ ($0.781$ vs.\ $0.752$; $\Delta = 0.029$), so we report the monotonic trend rather than a specific inflection point. Two of four generators---from distinct model families---show statistically significant improvement (both surviving Benjamini--Hochberg correction at $m{=}12$), ruling out generator-specific confounds.
16	
17	\begin{figure}[t]
18	\centering
19	\includegraphics[width=0.82\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}
20	\vspace{-3mm}
21	\caption{Combo gain vs.\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Gain decreases monotonically with generator capability (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$); significant gains persist into moderate negative gaps.}

23	\vspace{-6mm}
24	\end{figure}
25

1	\section{Background \& Setup}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}
5	\label{sec:condorcet}
6	
7	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \to \infty$ \citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:
8	\begin{equation}
9	\label{eq:effk}
10	K_{\text{eff}} = \frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \cdot \kappa}.
11	\end{equation}
12	In our experiments (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.2\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. $K_{\text{eff}}$ should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic---ranking which conditions have genuine vote diversity---rather than as a literal effective sample size. Fleiss' $\kappa$ is a chance-corrected agreement measure, not identical to the ICC ($\rho$) in \citeauthor{kish1965survey}'s (\citeyear{kish1965survey}) original formula; we validated their monotonic relationship empirically (mean pairwise $r = 0.625$ across model families). This \emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.
13	
14	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
15	\label{sec:pipeline}
16	
17	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
18	
19	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
20	
21	\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\% using true quantifiers. The \emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.
22	
23	\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:
24	\begin{equation}
25	\label{eq:maxsat}
26	\mathcal{C}^* = \arg\max_{\mathcal{S} \subseteq \mathcal{C}} \sum_{c \in \mathcal{S}} w_c \quad \text{s.t.} \quad \mathcal{S} \text{ is satisfiable.}
27	\end{equation}
28	Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\texttt{answer\_a} == \texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.
29	
30	\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\mathcal{C}^{\setminus} = \mathcal{C} \setminus \mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:
31	\begin{equation}
32	\label{eq:score}
33	\text{score}(a) = \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^*} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a) - \alpha \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^{\setminus}} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a),
34	\end{equation}
35	where $\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \arg\max_{a} \text{score}(a)$.
36	
37	\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:
38	\begin{equation}
39	\label{eq:degenerate}
40	\text{score}(a) \approx \sum_{\substack{c \in \mathcal{C}^* \\ \text{source}(c) \to a}} w_c,
41	\end{equation}
42	equivalent to majority voting. We show in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.
43	
44	\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\{q : |\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\}| / |\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC. The penalty weight $\alpha = 0.5$ is not a critical hyperparameter: accuracy varies by at most $0.49$\,pp across $\alpha \in \{0.0, 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\}$ on Mistral-7B FOLIO.
45	
46	\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}
47	\label{sec:mirage}
48	
49	We hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\S\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \emph{Bias Ratio}:
50	\begin{equation}
51	\label{eq:mirage}
52	\text{BR}(M,T) = \frac{P(c \text{ supports } a_M \mid a_M \neq a^*)}{P(c \text{ supports } a^* \mid a_M \neq a^*)},
53	\end{equation}
54	where $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}; Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Together with the oracle gap (\S\ref{sec:oracle}), BR bounds improvement: self-extracted constraints capture at most a $1/\text{BR}$ fraction of the oracle ceiling ($\Delta_{\max} \lesssim 6.0$\,pp for Mistral-7B, $\lesssim 3.6$\,pp for Qwen-14B; observed mean across conditions: $+2.12$\,pp). If chain-of-thought traces are unfaithful representations of the model's actual reasoning process \citep{turpin2024language}, self-extracted constraints inherit representation bias rather than reasoning content---making the mirage an inherent property of extraction from opaque models, not merely a quality issue. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\S\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
55	
```

I'm restructuring the Discussion to preserve the key insights: the two-channel mechanism in the opening, the BR gradient pattern across models, the degeneration problem in thinking mode, the ensemble tension citation, the Turpin faithfulness reference, and the Z3 semantic evaluation alternative alongside the semantic deduplication results. I want to tighten the prose and avoid redundantly restating numbers that already appear in the Analysis section. The bias ratio quantifies why self-verification fails: extraction and generation draw from identical distributional biases, so Z3 accepts 96.6% of constraints even when the model errs on 44% of problems—the issue is semantic, not syntactic. This bottleneck generalizes across model families and capability levels, affecting any self-verification approach whether it uses formal constraints, natural language critiques, or code assertions. On test-time scaling, effective samples plateau far below nominal budget; with K=12 we get K_eff between 1.20 and 3.62, and stronger models like Qwen3 produce nearly deterministic traces with κ≈0.91, suggesting sampling beyond a dozen iterations yields minimal new information.

For reasoning models in the o1/R1 paradigm, this creates a paradox: stronger models generate more consistent traces, which actually reduces the benefit of aggregating multiple samples from the same model when we need diversity most. Across four model families and three aggregation methods, κ stays clustered above 0.40 for standard approaches like self-consistency and diverse prompting, while Debate achieves lower κ only by sacrificing accuracy by 18.6 percentage points—same-model trace diversity has a hard ceiling that prompting strategies cannot overcome. This reflects the classic ensemble tension between independence and accuracy.

The independence-accuracy trade-off is fundamental: ensembles need members that are both individually accurate and mutually diverse, but a single model struggles to produce outputs that are simultaneously consistent and independent. Debate illustrates this perfectly—it boosts effective ensemble size from around 1.8 to 4.5, but only by degrading accuracy significantly, because adversarial perturbations to the model's generation process hurt its predictions rather than creating genuinely independent reasoning paths. Cross-architecture ensembles escape this trap because structurally different models like encoder-only and decoder-only architectures have different error profiles, achieving diversity without sacrificing individual accuracy. The core issue is whether traces actually reflect the model's reasoning or represent something more superficial.

An alternative approach using semantic evaluation through constraint solvers could theoretically avoid this degradation, but it runs into the same circular problem: evaluating whether a constraint supports an answer requires the model to judge its own outputs. For practitioners, I can identify two warning signs that symbolic augmentation will likely fail: if the baseline constraint satisfaction is below 5%, the augmented approach produces identical results to the baseline, and if it's at or below 40%, there's significant risk of error amplification. For models with intermediate performance and access to external verification, combining different architectures remains viable, with the magnitude of improvement predictable from the capability gap between them. Semantic deduplication using constraint solvers provides a modest but statistically significant boost.

The core insight is that formal verification adds value when redundancy is minimized, but the real bottleneck is extraction quality rather than verification itself. When constraints come from an oracle instead of the model's own outputs, the same mechanism that fails under self-extraction produces substantial gains, which sets an upper bound on what better extraction methods could achieve.

The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\\times$ LLaMA) confirms this scales with capability; thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) marks the upper bound. The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}. If traces do not reflect actual reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. An alternative $\\sigma(c, a)$ via Z3 semantic evaluation could in principle avoid degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), but faces circular dependency and converges at $\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$.",
          "+\\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}",
          "+The Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). This mechanism extends beyond formal extraction. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \\citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback, but their analyses remain qualitative. Our bias ratio ($4.97\\times$ Mistral, $3.93\\times$ Qwen, $2.88\\times$ LLaMA) supplies a quantitative explanation: self-extracted verification criteria systematically confirm the generating model's answer because extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints despite the model erring on ${\\sim}$44\\% of problems, confirming the bias is semantic---what gets extracted---rather than syntactic. The gradient $\\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether the channel is formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.",
          "+",
          "+\\paragraph{Test-time compute scaling.}",
          "+$K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling budgets beyond a dozen traces yield diminishing independent information. The pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$), indicating near-deterministic traces. For reasoning models in the o1/R1 paradigm \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, this creates a scaling paradox: stronger models produce more consistent traces, \\emph{reducing} the effectiveness of same-model aggregation precisely when it should matter most. The $\\kappa$ floor holds across four model families and three aggregation methods---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), while Debate achieves $\\kappa = 0.15$ only by collapsing accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp). Same-model trace diversity has a structural ceiling that no prompting strategy can break.",
          "+",
          "+\\paragraph{Ensemble diversity.}",
          "+The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: ensemble performance requires members that are individually accurate \\emph{and} mutually diverse, but a single model cannot simultaneously produce consistent and independent outputs. Debate demonstrates this: it raises $K_{\\text{eff}}$ from ${\\sim}1.8$ to ${\\sim}4.5$ by forcing adversarial exchanges, but sacrifices accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) because diversity-inducing perturbations to a single model's generation process degrade its predictions rather than introducing genuinely independent reasoning paths. Cross-architecture escape succeeds (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$) precisely because encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only LLMs possess structurally different error profiles, satisfying the diversity condition without sacrificing individual accuracy. If traces do not faithfully reflect the model's reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction from opaque models.",
          "+",
          "+\\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}",
          "+Two diagnostics predict when symbolic augmentation will fail before running the full pipeline: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA produces the same output as SC (degenerate regime); SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp on ProofWriter). For intermediate-SC models with access to an external verifier, cross-architecture combination is viable---gain magnitude is predicted by the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$), providing a principled selection criterion. An alternative scoring function $\\sigma(c, a)$ via Z3 semantic evaluation could in principle bypass degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), but faces the same circular dependency: semantically evaluating constraint--answer alignment requires the model to judge its own outputs. Z3 semantic deduplication yields a small but significant improvement ($p = 0.013$; \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}), confirming the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced; the bottleneck is not verification but extraction quality. The same mechanism that fails under self-extraction succeeds with oracle constraints ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp), bounding the prize for better extraction methods."
        ]
      }
    ],

24	Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck persists at the 7B--32B scale.
25	Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case. We show that even formal verification inherits the model's bias, revealing process-level homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.
26	
27	Oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \S\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling.
28	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp; other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling across three families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract logical constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nTwenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck persists at the 7B--32B scale.\nPrior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \\emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case. We show that even formal verification inherits the model's bias, revealing process-level homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-seven remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier yields significant gains on two of four generators across model families ($+3.50$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $p{=}0.013$; $+4.17$\\,pp LLaMA-3.1-8B, $p{=}0.0035$; both at $w{=}3$), suggesting the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four models from three families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) in every case; the gap generally decreases with capability but varies across families, and process homogeneity persists across all architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",

64	\end{table*}
65	
66	None of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation (uncorrected McNemar $p < 0.05$): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} ($\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for 5 conditions; 5 show genuine differences (4 negative); 6 are underpowered (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). We trace the failure to three mechanisms in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\S\ref{sec:remediation}.
67	
68	
69	\subsection{Oracle Control}
70	\label{sec:oracle}
71	
72	Oracle variants replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations: logical formulae from FOLIO ($n = 204$) and DSL from ProofWriter ($n = 600$). Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \times 10^{-7}$), and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\% (ProofWriter) of problems. The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted result ($+3.43$\,pp) captures only 11.5\% of its oracle ceiling, with ${\sim}$88\% lost to extraction quality.
73

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models on three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations across ${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances. Beyond accuracy comparisons, we conduct diagnostic analyses of symbolic confirmation bias and process-level trace independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$; extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$. Z3 timeout: 10s. $\\alpha{=}0.5$. Statistical significance via McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs from 10,000 resamples for $n \\geq 200$.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational cost.} On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($K{=}12$), SICA takes $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock time; constraint extraction ($K$ additional LLM calls) accounts for 76\\% of overhead, Z3 solving is negligible (${<}0.1\\%$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline. We identify three empirical regimes (thresholds observation-driven, not theoretically derived). SICA effectiveness depends sharply on this accuracy regime.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all conditions sorted by SC accuracy. Results hold across the full capability range: from Mistral-7B (55.88\\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86.27\\% SC), SICA delivers no significant gain. $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). $^b$In-context learning with gold logical examples. $\\dagger$\\,$n < 100$ (suggestive only). \\textbf{Bold $\\Delta$}: $p < 0.05$ (McNemar). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$);\\enspace{***}\\,$p < 0.001$ (oracle). Failure modes detailed in Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}. $^{4s}$\\,4-seed replication (confirmatory); unmarked single-seed conditions are exploratory.}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--55\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & 59.31 & $+$3.43 & 0.092 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 54.41 & 53.92 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 75.17 & 72.67 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & 84.31 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & $+$0.67 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Degenerate ($C_0 < 5\\%$)}} \\\\\nQwQ-32B & AIME 2024$^\\dagger$ & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \\textbf{85.78} & $\\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\\times}10^{-13}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \\textbf{89.71} & $\\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\\times}10^{-7}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 75.17 & \\textbf{86.67} & $\\mathbf{+11.50}$ & $9.21{\\times}10^{-15}${***} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\nNone of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation (uncorrected McNemar $p < 0.05$): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} ($\\pm 2$\\,pp) establish equivalence for 5 conditions; 5 show genuine differences (4 negative); 6 are underpowered (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). We trace the failure to three mechanisms in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\nOracle variants replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations: logical formulae from FOLIO ($n = 204$) and DSL from ProofWriter ($n = 600$). Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter) of problems. The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted result ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling, with ${\\sim}$88\\% lost to extraction quality.\n\n\n",

The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality and independence of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nSelf-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints on average, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts logical constraints consistent with it. Constraints use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% zero cross-answer variable overlap), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all and aggregation reduces to weighted majority voting. Confirmation bias generalizes across families: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all exceeding $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Bias intensifies along reasoning steps: late-step constraints (step $\\geq 7$) exhibit 27.2\\% conclusion leakage vs.\\ 22.0\\% for early steps (Fisher $p = 3 \\times 10^{-6}$); SICA restricted to late-only constraints degrades by $-10.3$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the gap ranges from $0.31$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) to $0.02$ (Qwen2.5-14B), broadly decreasing with model capability but with notable variation across families. Answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant improvement ($p = 0.013$), indicating that the formal verification mechanism itself adds value---but this gain is overwhelmed by confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nTwenty-seven remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}",
